New Delhi , the 28 th May 1968 

S.O. 1964 — In pursuance of section 17 of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 
(14 of 1947), the Central Government hereby publishes the following award of 
the Industrial Tribunal, Dhanbad, in the industrial dispute between the employers 
In relation to the East Ghanoodih Colliery of Messrs East Ghanoodlh Colliery 
Company (Private) Limited, Post Office Jharia, District Dhanbad and their work- 
men, which was received by the Central Government on the 22nd May, 1968. 

BEFORE THE CENTRAL GOVERNMENT INDUSTRIAL TRIBUN AL-CUM- 
LABOUR COURT (NO. 1), DHANBAD- 

In the matter of a reference Under Section 10(1) (d) of the Industrial 
Disputes Act, 1947. 

Reference No. 65 of 1967. 

Parties ; 

Employers in relation to the East Ghanoodih Colliery of Messrs Eiw 
Ghanoodih Colliery Company (Private) Limited, P. 0- Jharia, 
District Dhanbad- 


And 

Their Workmen- 
Present : 

Shri Kamla Sahai.— -Presiding Officer- 
Appearances : 

For the Employers ■ — Shri B, P- Dabral, Chief Personnel Officer of the 
Company. 

For the Workmen . — Shri B. K- Lath, Advocate. 

STATE; Bihar. INDUSTRY; Coal- 

Dhanbad, dated the 15 th May, 1968- 


AWARD 

The Government of India has, by its Order No. 2/124/67-LRII, dated 18th 
November, 1967, referred this industrial dispute for adjudication to this Tribunal- 
The schedule attached to the reference is as follows: — 

Schedule 

"Whether the management of the East Ghanoodih Colliery of Messrs East 
Ghanoodih Colliery Company (Private) Limited, Post Office Jharia 
(Dhanbad) was justified in, not allowing employment to Sarvashrl 
Adhin Singh and Gangadhar Singh, Mine Loaders with effect from 
the 14th June, 1967? If not, to what relief are the workmen entitled?” 

2. Both the parties have agreed that the name of the Colliery does not begin 
with “EAST" but It is simply Ghanoodih Colliery, East Section. The description 
given by the parties In agreement with each other may be assumed to be correct. 


3. The workmen, Shri Adhin Singh and Shri Gangadhar Singh represented by 
the General Secretary, Krant.ikarl Koyala Mazdoor Sangh, filed a written statement 
on 30th December, 1907. They stated therein that the workmen were permanent 
employers of the Colliery and had put In a number of years of service, that the 
workmen were arbitrarily stopped from their duties on 14th June, 1967 without 
any Charge-Sheet and without assigning any reason and that the workmen have 
been victimized for their Union activities. 

4, Tbe employers filed a written statement , on 17th January, 1968 when they 
had not yet received the written statement filed on behalf of the Union. In that 
written statement, they stated that the Reference was misconceived and bad In 
law, that the workmen concerned had no locus standi to raise the dispute in-as- 
much as they were not workmen under the Industrial Disputes Act on the date 
of the dispute, that Gangadhar Singh was appointed on 30th January 1987 as a 
Machine Loader on a permanent substantive post as a probationer but he absented 
himself with effect from 13th June, 1907 before the expiry of his probationary 
period and hence h e was treated as having abandoned his service and that Adhin 



.Sue. 3(11)] THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JUNE 3, 1968/JVA1STHA IS, 1090 


268 r 


Singh was appointed on 3rd February, 1967 as a Machine Loader on a permanent 
substantive post as a probationer but he started absenting himself with effect 
from 30th May, 1967 and hence he was also treated as having abandoned his 
service. 

.. 5 - another written statement filed by the employers on 30th January, 1968, 
they stated that they have never been aware of the Union activities of the workmen 
and that there is, therefore, no question of their being victimized for such activities- 

6. At the hearing, the parties have filed some documents and they have also 
examined some witnesses. 


7. The employers having admitted that Gangadhar Singh was appointed on 
permanent substantive post with effect from 30th January, 1967 and Adhln Singh 
was appointed on permanent substantive post with effect from 3rd February 1967 
the first question which arises for consideration is whether the appointment of 
the two workmen was on probation for a particular period. The letters of 
appointment of the workmen have not been produced nor have the office copies 
ot those letters been produced- Shri Vikramaditya Jha (M W. 2) has produced 
a Register of employees (Ext. M2). The name of Gangadhar Singh appears In 
this Register- The witness has stated that Ext. M-2(a) is the thumb impression 
of Gangadhar Singh, but that fact is disputed. Besides, the Register does not 
bear the signature of the Manager Or the Assistant Manager on any page. Shri 
B ' j who has appeared on behalf of the workmen, has pointed out that 

under Clause 41(6) of the Coal Mines Regulations, 1957, the Manager has to 
examine all registers among other records which are to be kept under the Act 
and has to countersign the signature on the Register though he may delegate this 
duty by an order in writing to an undermanager. Apart from the defect of 
absence of the signature of the manager or undermanager, the alleged thumb 
impression [Ext. M-2(a)] is so faint and tmderclpherable that, it cannot be accepted 
as a genuine thumb-impression of Gangadhar Singh- In any case, there is 
nothing in this Register to show that Gangadhar Singh was appointed on preba- 
tlon. No other document has been filed to prove that either Gangadhar Singh 
or Adhln Srngh was appointed on probation, M-W. 2 has said nothing on this 
point. Shri S. V. Krishnamurty, (M.W. 1) has been working as the Manager of 
Ghanoodih Colliery, East Section, from June, 1963. He says that appointment 
is made in his Colliery is the first instance o n . a temporary, badlt or probation 
basis, but he has not escorted that either Gangadhar Singh or Adhln Singh was 
appointed on probation. I am, therefore, unable to hold that either of those 
workmen was on probation on, 0 r about the date on which each of them is alleged 
to have abandoned or been stopped from service- 


8. Shri Gangadhar Singh has examined himself as W-W. 1. He has stated 
that he has been working In the colliery from 1964 but previously he was on a 
temporary basis. It may be that, as shown by the register Ext. M-2, he was put 
on a permanent basis from 30fh January, 1967. In that case also, he could not 
be put on probation at that stage. 


9- Admittedly, no charge was framed and no enquiry was held against the 
workmen in question. The question for consideration which, therefore, arises at 
this stage is whether a workman can cease to be in service simply because he 
absents himself from his duties without any further action being taken by the 
employers. ' In other words, can a workman be simply Ignored or can he be 
deemed to have been thrown out of service unceremoniously because he absents 
himself for a day or two? Shri Krishnamurty— M-W, 1— says that when a 
workman starts absenting himself regularly, the Register-Keeper make? a note 
that he is no longer in service- This is a curious procedure. A workman cannot 
be deemed to have abandoned service simply because of his absence for a day 
or two Realising this situation, M-W. 1 added that the Register-Keeper makes 
enquiry from the Manager whether the workman is or Is not in service and then 
the Manager gives him the correct position. 


10. The Manager (M-W. 1) says that the first news which he got about the 
grievance of Gangadhar Singh was from the communication (Ext. M-3) from the 
A.L.C.(C). This is dated 29th August, 1067. Had the services of the workmen 
in question been terminated with effect from 14th June, 1967 because of their 
voluntary absence, surely he must have learnt about it before 29th August, 1967 
I, therefore, hold that the workmen did not voluntary absent themselves from 
service. 


11- This may be considered from another aspect Clause 13 of the Company’s 
Standing Orders (Ext- M-l) provides that if an employee remains absent without 



2682 


THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JUNE 9, 1968/JYAISTHA 18, 1890 [Part II- 


properly approved leave he will render himself liable for disciplinary action. If 
the two workmen in question had made themselves liable for such disciplinary 
action, charge should have been framed against them, enquiry should have been 
made and formal orders of termination of service should have been passed against 
them, The Manager — M.W- 1— has stated that he did not issue a notice to 
Gangadhar Singh or Adhln Singh terminating their service on the ground of 
abandonment of services by them and that, so far as he remembers, no charge- 
sheet was issued to them- 

12. In the circumstances mentioned above, I accept the workmen’s case that 
the management stopped them from their work from 14th June, 1967- 

13. Shri Dabral, who has appeared on behalf of the employers, has argued 
that the counterfoil receipt — Ext- W- 1— is not genuine and that the workmen 
have not proved that they are members of the Union known as Krantikari Koyala 
Mazdoor Sangh, much less that the Management of the Company knew of their 
activities as members of that Union. It is unnecessary to consider thiB argument 
because, as I have already held, the management stopped them from working and 
this the management was not entitled to do- 

14- In view of what I have said above, I am clearly of the opinion that the 
management of the Colliery Company in' question was not justified in not allowing 
employment to Sarvashri Adhln Singh and Gangadhar Singh, Machine Loaders, 
with effect from 14th June, 1987 Tney are, therefore, entitled to be re-instated 
as Machine Loaders with effect from 14th June, 1907 with their back wages and 
continuity of service. 

15. This is my award. Let It be submitted to the Central Government under 
Section IS of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947- 

Sd./- Kamla Sahai, 

Presiding Officer, 
Central Govt- Industrial Tribunal-cum-Labour 
Court, No. 1, Dhanbad. 

[No. 2/124/67-LRII.J

(14 ol 1947), the Central Government hereby publishes the folowing award of the 
Central Govern mint Industrial Tribunal, Bombay, in the industrial dispute between 
the employers in relation to the management of Messrs Hindustan Construction 
Company, Post Office Pamba, District Kottayam and their workmen, which was 
received by the Central Government on the 21st May, 1968. 

BEFORE THE CENTRAL GOVERNMENT INDUSTRIAL TRIBUNAL, BOMB A Y- 

Reference No- CGIT-69 of 1906 

Parties : 

Employers In relaion to Messrs, Hindustan Construction Company Ltd., 
Sabarlgiri Project- 


And 

Their Workmen- 
Present : 

Shri A. T. Zambre, Presiding Officer. 

Appearances : 

For the Employers — Shri K. V R. Shenoi of Messrs. Menon and Pa. 

Advocates and Shri B. Narayanaswamy, Advocate- 
For the Workmen . — No appearance- 

STATE: Kerala- INDUSTRY: Stone Quarrying 


Dated 26 th April, 1908 
AWARD 

The Government of India in the Ministry of Labour and Employment have by 
their order No- 36/33/05-LRI dated 20th November, 1906 referred to this tribunal 
for adjudication the industrial dispute existing between the employers in relation 
to the stone quarry of Messrs. Hindustan Construction Company, Pattanamthitta, 
District Quilon and their workmen in respect of the matters specified in the 
following schedule: — 


Schedule 

“(1) Whether the workmen employed in the stone quarry at Pattanamthiti,^, 
District Quilon of Messrs- Hindustan Construction Company Limited 
are entitled for bonus for the years 1002-1963 and 1963-1904? 

(2) If so, to what extent?’' 

2- The employers — the Hindustan Construction Company Ltd., were engaged 
in the work of construction of a dam and a tunnel at Sabarlgiri- In connection 
with the dam work there was an adjacent stone quarry in which the employer 
company had engaged about 388 workmen. The construction of the dam started 
In the year 1962 and was continuing. It was expected to be completed In the 
year I960- The workmen employed by the company including the stone quarry 
workers had through the Secretary of the Sabarlgiri Project, Construction Em- 
ployees’ Union sent a chartsr of demands about bonus, onam advance, dearness 
allowance, issue of service cards etc,, and the matter was also represented to the 
Conciliation Officer- Negotiations were held and there was a settlement regarding 
almost all the demands including the bonus dispute- By the settlement the 
management agreed to pay bonus to all the workmen including the employees 
engaged in the stone quarry equivalent to ten days wages per year in full and 
final settlement of the claim. But subsequently the union again raised the same 
question about bonus for the years 1962-63 and 1963-64 In relation to the quarry 
workers and it has been referred to this tribunal. 

3- The employers have by their written statement contended that in respect 
of the present dispute conciliation proceedings were held before the Deputy Labour 
Officer and Conciliation Officer, Pattanamthitta who was camping at Kakki In the 
presence of the representatives of the employers and the workmen and pursuant 
Lo the discussion that took place at that conciliation meeting a settlement was 



Si r. 3(ii)l THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JUNE 8, 1%8/JYATSTUA 18, 1800 


arrived at. As per the settlement with reference 1 to the demand for bonus for 
the years 1962-63 and 1963-64 it was agreed that all the workmen should be paid 
bonus equivalent to 10 days wages (consolidated) per year in respect of the said 
two years in full and final settlement of the claim of the workers for bonus. 
Pursuant to the said settlement the company even paid the agreed bonus to all 
Its workmen both in the dam. tunnel work and stone 1 quarry and all the workers 
without any exception have received the payment of this agreed bonus. They 
have contended that thus the demand for bonus in respect of the years 1962-63 
and 1963-64 have been fully and finally settled as Per the settlement dated 16th 
June, 1965- The workers were also paid the agreed amounts and they are not 
entitled to any adltional amount of bonus for the said two years. 

4- After the receipt of the reference notices were Issued to the General 
Secretary, Sabarigiri Project Construction Employees’ Union P. O- R. Perunad, 
District Koltayam, Kerala dated 4th December, 1965, 20th December, 1966, 29th 
January, 1966, 26th February, 1966, 3rd March, 1966, 23rd March, 1967 and 14th 
February, 1968- These notices were received by the General Secretary of the 
Sabarigiri Project Construction Employees’ Union but he has neither put In any 
appearance nor filed any statement of claim. Once by his application dated 16th 
June, 1966 the General Secretary of the Union had requested for extension of 
time to file the statement of claim on behalf of the workers and though time was 
granted no written statement of claim has been filed so far- The office had also 
forwarded to the Secretary of the Union a copy of the written statement of the 
company along with the letter dated 29th March, 1966. The same has been 
received by the Secretary of the union but he has not taken any steps to file any 
statement of claim. 

5- Notice of the hearing of this dispute in this sitting of the Tribunal at 
Ernakulam has been duly received by the General Secretary of the Union but he 
has remained absent. It is clear from the record of the conciliation proceedings 
that the workmen were represented by the Sabarigiri Project. Construction 
Employees’ Union P. O- R. Perunad, District Quilon, Kerala before the Conciliation 
Officer, The order of reference shows that the General Secretary, Sabarigiri 
Project Construction Employees’ Union, P, O- R- Perunad, District Kottayam. 
Kerala represented the workmen concerned in this reference about the demand 
for bonus for the two years in question and the notices were issued to the General 
Secretary of the Sabarigiri Project Construction Employees' Union to the address 
mentioned in the order of reference- It is also clear from the record that the 
notices issued from the office of the Tribunal have been received by the union and 
althouogh the notice of hearing on 4th March, 1968 has been duly received by the 
General Secretary of the union he has remained absent. In spite of the notice dated 
8th April, 1968 and the hearing dated 22nd April, 1968 the union did not appear nor 
did it put forth any written statement and considering the conduct of the Union in 
not appearing and taking part it appears that they are not interested in the proceed- 
ings of the reference and hence the matter is heard ex-parte- 

6- The employers have by their written statement contended that the dispute 
regarding the demand for bonus for the years 1962-63 and 1963-64 between 
themselves and the workmen Including the stone quarry labourers has been 
settled during the conciliation proceedings- It was agreed by them to pay to all 
the workmen bonus equivalent to 10 days wages per year in respect of the said 
two years in full and final settlement of the claim. Along with the written 
statement they have produced a copy of the memorandum of settlement dated 
16th June, 1965 which purports to have been arrived at during the conciliation 
proceedings before the Conciliation Officer, Pattanamthitta camp Kakki, In the 
terms of settlement the very first Item 19 regarding bonus which states: — 

“ 1 ■ Bonus. — It is agreed that all workmen shall be paid bonus equivalent 
to 10 days wages per year in full and final settlement of the claim 
of the workers for bonus-” 


The employers In their written statement have stated that according to these terms 
of settlement they have made payments of the agreed amounts of bonus to the 
workers including the stone quarry workers- The union has not filed any written 
statement and there Is no reason to disbelieve the company’s contention in the 
absence of any denial on the part of the workmen. It Is clear from the copy ol 
the settlement that the dispute has been settled and it appears that because of 
this settlement the workers are nof interested and do not want to take part in 
the present proceedings- 



2686 


THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JUNE 8, 1968/JYAISTHA 18, 1890 [Part II- 


7- As per the provisions of Rule 20 of the Industrial Disputes (Central Rules, 
1957, In cases where the workmen who are members of any trade union or associa- 
tion are parties to the dispute the service of notice on the Secretary on on the 
principal officer of the union or association shall be deemed to be service on such 
persons. The Secretary has not remained present and has not shown any cause 
for his failure to file any written statement or attend the proceedings. Rule 22 
of the Industrial Disputes (Central) Rules, 1957, enables the authority to hear 
proceedings ex p arte. It says: — 

“If without sufficient cause being shown, any party to proceedings before 
a Board, Court, Labour Court, Tribunal, National Tribunal or 
Arbitrator fails to attend or to be represented the Board, Court, Labour 
Court, Tribunal, National Tribunal or Arbitrator may proceed as if 
the party had duly attended or had been represented.” 


8. The learned Counsel for the employers has contend 3d that the reference 
pertains to the demand of bonus for the years 1962-03 and 1963-64- Their Lord- 
ships of the Supreme Court have struck down the provisions of section 33 of the 
Payment of Bonus Act, 1905 which had enabled the workers to obtain bonus for 
the two years before the introduction of the act under the Act and as the workmen 
know full well that they will not be entitled to claim any bonus for the two years 
and hence they have remained absent. Shri Narayanaswamy the learned 
Advocate on behalf of the management has further argued that as there was a 
settlement arrived at between the parties before this reference the management 
ha3 already paid the bonus to the workmen involved In this dispute. They have 
taken advantage of the settlement and they are not now entitled to claim anything 
further. It has been further argued that in spite of the several notices issued to 
the union they have not put forth any statement of claim which clearly showed 
that the workmen were satisfied with the bonus received by them under the 
agreement and they are not entitled to claim anything for the same years. 


9- I have already referred to the various notices issued to the union -egarding 
the hearing of this reference but the unioh has not appeared and has not filed 
any statement and it appears that due to the settlement of the dispute before the 
Conciliation Officer the union and workmen are now satisfied and do not want to 
proceed with this demand- The copy of the memorandum of settlement between 
the parties which has been produced by the employers along with their written 
statement supports the management that the parties had agreed to the payment of 
bonus equivalent to ten days wages per year in full and final settlement of the 
claim and it appears that after receiving the amount of 10 days’ wages as bonus 
the union again reiterated the same demand before the Conciliation Officer and I 
do not think that the same demand will now be justified- The copy of the settle- 
ment shows that the settlement had taken place before the Conciliation Officer. 
It Is signed both by the management and the representatives of the workers. It 
Is also signed bv the Conciliation Officer ‘and there appears no reason why the same 
should not be accepted- In spite of the several notices the union has not remained 
present. Clearly the workmen are satisfied with the settlement and now they have no 
claim of any kind for the bonus for the two years 1962-63 and 1983-64. It is also 
clear that the provisions of section 33 of the Payment of Bonus Act have been 
struck down by their Lordships of the Supreme Court in the case of Jalan 
Trading Co. Ltd., v. Mill Mazdoor Sabha reported in I960 II LLJ 546- The 
workmen are not entitled to claim bonus for the two years under the Payment of 
Bonus Act and hence also the union has not taken part in the proceedings. The 
workmen employed in the stone quarry are not entitled to bonus for the two years 
1962-63 and 1963-04 and hence my award accordingly. 


No order as to costs. 


Sd./- A. T. Zambbe, 

Presiding Officer- 

Central Government Industrial Tribunal, Bombay. 

[No- 36/33/65-LRL]

AWARD 

By Order No- 28(36)/06-LRIV dated 17th March 1966 the Government of. India 
In the Ministry of Labour and Employment has referred to this Tribunal the 
industrial dispute between the employers in relation to Messrs. Darragh Smail and 
Company (India) Ltd., Cochin-3 and their workmen in respect of the matters spe- 
cified in the following schedule:— 


Schedule 

“Whether the steamer tally clerks employed by M/s. Darragh Smail & Com- 
pany (India) Ltd., Cochin-3, arg entitled for payment of bonus under 
the Payment of Bonus Act, 1965 for the accounting year ending on 31st 
March 1965. If so, to what extent and to what other relief, if any, 
are they entitled?’’. 

2. The management has by its written statement opposed the claim and has 
contended that the steamer tally clerks were never the employees of the company. 
There was no employer-employee relationship between them and they are not entit- 
led to any bonus- They have denied that the working results of the company will 
enable bonus to be paid at 20 per cent and have contended that at any rate it 
should be considered on the basis of the accounts of the company which would 
be produced at the appropriate time. 

3. No statement of claim has been filed on behalf of the workmen in this refer- 
ence. 

4. The reference was made to this Tribunal on 17th March 1966 and after the 
issue of notices and the filing of statements the matter was adjourned from time 
to time. Ultimately in this sitting of the Tribunal at Ernakulam the parties nego- 
tiated the dispute and settled it amicably and filed a joint memorandum of settle- 
ment under which the management has agreed to pay to the tally clerks bonus for 
the year ending on 31st March 1966, The joint memorandum of settlement has 
been produced by the parties at annexure ‘A’- It is clear from the terms of settle- 
ment that the tally clerks are to get bonus at the rate at which it was paid by the 
company to the head load labourers employed at Willingdon Island. The terms are 
reasonable and hence I pass an award In terms of the joint memorandum of settle- 
ment annexure ’A’ which shall from part of this award. 

No order as to costs. 

Sd/- A. T, Zambre, 

Presiding Officer,

AWARD 

By Order No. 28/135/64-LRIV dated 6th February 1965 the Government of 
India in the Ministry of Labour and Employment have referred to this tribunal 
the industrial dispute existing between the employers In relation to Shri N. P. 
Nayak, Customs House Agent, Cochin 2 and their workmen in respect of the 
matters specified in the following schedule: — 

Schedule 

“Whether the action of Shri N. P, Nayak, Customs House Agent, Cochin 2 
in I'efusing employment to Shri B, L. Mallia, as Tally Clerk with 
effect from 23rd November 1964 is justified? If not to what relief Is 
the workmen entitled?” 

2, The Steamer Tally Clerks Union which represents ihe workman Shri B. L. 
Mallia has by its statement to claim alleged that Shri Mallia was in the employ 
of Shri N. p. Nayak, Customs House Agent and was working regularly as a tally 
clerk for the last several years. As the management delayed payment of wages, 
Shri Mallia had to file a suit against the management for the delayed wages- This 
suit S.C. 266 of 1960 was compromised. 



269’V ’ THE GAZET TE OF INDIA : JC\E 8, I9fi8/J-Y.USTHA 18. 1890 [I’aki 11 


The union has alleged that though the suit was settl xl the management was 
waiting for an opportunity to victimise Shri Mallla and in the year 1964 they again 
began to cut his wages and delay the payments. Shri lYiallia raised a complaint 
about it. However the management in retaliation stopped employing him from 
23rd November 1964 without assigning any reason- Hence Shri Mallia who was 
a member of the union approached the union who raised a dispute- It has been 
further alleged that Shri Mallia has been refused employment from 23rd November 
1964 and from that date till 29th March 19(15 he would have been able to earn 
wages amounting to Rs. 282-40. Hence the union has prayed for a direction for 
his re-employment and claimed wages amounting to Rs. 282:40. 

3. The company by its written statement has denied the claim of the workman 
and has contended that Shri Mallia was not employed in the service of the manage- 
ment as such and there was no employer-employee relationship between them- 
Thcre was no industrial dispute ivithin the meaning of the Industrial Disputes Act 
and Shri Mallia was not entitled to claim anything from them. They have acl 
mitted that the services of Shri Mallia wore utilised by them for the purpose of 
some tally but have contended that on each of such occasions he was acting 
as an independent person. He used to submit his bills in respect of each such 
occasion and as each separate tally work was a separate and independent piece of 
work at the worst there was only an engagement of Shri Mallia by the manage- 
ment on a contractual basis for finishing each item of work and the contractual 
relationship ceased immediately the work was done- This did not constitute Shri 
Mallia an employee of the management and there was no relationship of employer 
and employee- lie was not entitled to claim any loss of wages from the company. 

4. After the receipt of this reference on 2nd March 1965 and the statements of 
the parties the matter was adjourned for hearing from time to time. But during 
this sitting of the Tribunal at Ernakulam the parties negotiated the dispute and 
have settled it amicably. They have filed a compromise petition at annexure ‘A’, 
According to the terms of the compromise the management has agreed to pay a 
sum of Rs. 200 to Shri Mallia in full and final payment of all the bills submitted 
by him and the claim for compensation made in this reference. The union by its 
statement of claim had demanded Rs. 282'40 and considering the terms of compro- 
mise it shall have to bo held that the compromise is reasonable. I accept the same 
and pass an award in terms of Hie jomt settlement annexure ‘A’ which shall from 
part of this award. 

No order as to costs. 


Sd/- A- T. Z/imhue; 

Presiding Officer,

<of 1947), the Central Government hereby publishes the following award of the 
Industrial Tribunal, Bombay in the Industrial Dispute between the employers in 
-relation to the Indo Ceylon Trading Company, Cochin and their workmen, which 
was received by the Central Government on 21st May 1968. 

BEFORE THE CENTRAL GOVERNMENT INDUSTRIAL TRIBUNAL. BOMBAY. 

Reference No. CGIT-49 of 1968 

Parties : 

Employers in relation to the Indo Ceylon Trading Company, Cochin. 

And 

THEIR WORKMEN:— 

Present ;

Appearances • 

For the employers- -Shrl P. Balakrlshna Menon, Advocate. 

For the workmen, — Shri P. F. Thomas, Advocate and President, Port and 
Steamer Workmen’s Association. 

State: Kerala- Industry: Major Ports and Docks. 

Bombay, the -iQth April 1968 
AWARD 

The Government of India in the Ministry ol Labour Employment and Rehabi- 
litation (Department of Labour & Employment) have by their Order No. 28(96)/ 
66-LRIV dated 15th September 1966 referred to this Tribunal an industrial dispute 
existing between the employers in relation to the Indo Ceylon Trading Company 
Ltd-, and their workmen in respect of the matters specified in the following sche- 
dule: — 


Schedule 

“Whether the Indo Ceylon Trading Company, Steamer Agents, Cochin-2, are 
justified in refusing employment to Shri Donald Clements, Cargo, 
Supervisor on board the Country Crafts from 14th March 1968° If 
not, to what relief is the workmen entitled?” 

2. The employers, the indo-Ceylon Trading Co- Ltd., are steamer agents and 
commission agents with their office at Cochin. They are also agents for the "Maldi- 
vian Nationals Trading Corporation (Ceylon) Ltd,, anrl act as representatives of 
the Maldivian Government Trade Agents. The management had engaged the 
workmen Shri Donald as a cargo supervisor and he was in their employ he gave 
him employment upto 1.4th March 1966 but subsequently denied employment to 
him and hence the Port and Steamer Workmen's Association of which he was a 
member made representations against the management as a result of which this 
dispute was raised. 



2694 


THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JUNE 8, 1%8/JtYAISTHA 18. 1890 [Part II- 


The Association has by its statement of claim contended that Shri Donald 
Clements was working with the management as a cargo supervisor for the steamers 
arriving at Cochin for the last nine years. His work was supervised and control- 
led by the management and they used to pay him his remuneration at scheduled 
rates. Shri Donald Clements also worked with the management as a cargo super- 
visor of the cargo loaded and unloaded at: Cochin Port from the Maldivian country 
crafts arriving at Cochin Harbour and he did this work continuously for the last 
three years. He was efficient and dutiful but the management refused him emp- 
loyment on the country craft S, V. Deefarum on 14th March 1966 and also subse- 
quently without assigning any reason. When he as usual went to the office he 
was illegally denied employment. The management engaged their office clerk for 
the supervision work of the other countrycrafts which arrived at the Cochin Port 
and as Shri Donald Clements was illegally denied employment he was entitled to 
get his wages during the period and as the refusal of" work’ was not justified Shri 
Donald Clements is entitled to reinstatement and the wages during the period he 
was refused employment. 

The company In its statement In reply has denied the claim of the Association. 
They have admitted that they had employed Shri Donald Clements as a super- 
visor but have contended that he was engaged as and when the company required 
his services on payment of daily rate of wages. They have contended that they 
were not technically the agents for loading and unloading for the country crafts 
which called at Cochin and the company had to act as per the Tindal’s (Master’s) 
requirements. The country crafts did not come under the purview of the Dock 
Labour Board for supply of labour. The country crafts make their own arrange- 
ments by employing ships’ crew or recruiting labour from shore. The crafts 

mentioned In the dispute advised the company that they did not require any 

labour or tally or supervision work' and therefore the company did not arrange 

for labour and the services of Shri Donald were not required. As Shri Donald 

was not In the regular employ of the company there was no question of any 
refusal of employment and he was not entitled to get any relief or wages. 

After the statements of claim and the reply and rejoinder were filer! by the 
parties the matter was adjourned from time to time. At the time of this sitting 
of the Tribunal at Ernakulam the parlies negotiated the dispute and settled the 
seme amicably and filed a memorandum of settlement at annexure ‘A’. By this 
settlement the management has agreed to employ Shri Donald Clements on country 
crafts as before and has further agreed to pay him Rs- 76/- towards his wages. 
The settlement is reasonable and I accept the same and make an award In terms 
of the joint memorandum of settlement annexure ‘A’ which shall form part of 
this award. 

No order as to costs. 


Sd./- A. T. Zambtu:. 

Presiding Officer, 
Central Government Industrial, 
Tribunal, Bombay. 


Annerure ’A’

S.O. 1981. — In pursuance of Section 17 of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1047 
(14 of 1947), the Central Government hereby publishes the following award of 
the Industrial Tribunal, Bombay In the Industrial Dispute between the employers 
in relation to M/s- Paul Abrao and Sons, Cochin and their workmen, which was 
received by the Central Government on 1st May, 1968. 

BEFORE THE CENTRAL GOVERNMENT INDUSTRIAL, TRIBUNAL, BOMBAY-

State: Kerala Industry: Major Ports and Docks. 

Bombay 30 th April 1968. 

AWARD 

By Order No. 28(30) /66-LRIV dated 1st April, 1966 the Government of India 
in the Ministry- of Labour, Employment and Rehabilitation, have referred to this 
Tribunal an Industrial dispute existing between the employers In relation to 
Messrs Paul Abrao and Sons, Cochin-3 and their workmen represented (by the 
Cochin Thuramugha Thozhilali Union in respect of the matter set forth in the 
following Schedule: — 

Schedule 

“Whether the coal handling workers employed by M/s. Paul Abrao. and 
Sons, Cochln-3 are entitled to the benefits given under the interim 
recommendations of the Central Wage Board for Port, and Dock 
Workers? If so to what extent, and from what date.” 

The employers Messrs Paul Abrao and Sons are working as coal handling 
contractors of the Southern Railway at Willingdon Island, Cochin-3. The work- 
men in their employment had raised a disputo through their union viz., the Cochin 
Thuramugha Thozhilali Union for the interim relief recommendation by the Cen- 
tral Wage Board for Port and Dock Workers and this dispute has been by the 
joint application of the employers and the workmen has been referred to this 
Tribunal for decision by the order dated 1st 'April, 1966. 

After the receipt of reference notices dated 25th April 1986, 14th February 
1968 were issued to the parties. In spite of these notices neither the employers nor 
the office bearers of the union filed any statements of claim or reply. On the 
4th March, 1968 when the dispute came up for hearing at Ernakulam the emp- 
loyers though served were absent. The General Secretary of the Cochin Thura- 
mugha Thozhilali Union Shri A. A. Kochunny M 10 was present submitted to the 




2696 1 HE GAZETTE OF INDIA; JUNE 8 , I968/JIYAJSTHA 18,. J 890 JPaju ,11- 


Tribunal that the dispute between ^he workmen and the employers was compro- 
mised and the union did not want to press the Issue- On that day he had also 
filed a written application to that effect . With a view to hear the employers the 
matter was then adjourned and again notices were issued to the management 
and the union for hearing on 22nd April, 1968 at Ernakulam. At this hearing the 
representatives of both parties appeared. They submitted that the matter has 
been settled and the union did not want t o pr ess its claim for interim relief. As 
the matter has been settled by the parties and the union does not press the claim 
it shall have to be held that the workers have settled the dispute and they are not 
entitled to the relief claimed and hence my award accordingly. 

No order as to costs, 

(Sd/)- A. T. Zambre, 

Presiding Officer, 
Central Government Industrial, 
Tribunal, Bombay,

AWARD 

This is a complaint by one Shri C. Velu of Eraveli, Cochin- 1 against his 
employers Shri A, K. Muhamedkutty, Boat Owner and Contractor under seel ion 
33A of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947- 

2. The complainant has alleged that he was employed by the opposite pai'ty 
as a tyndal for the last four years and was in their continuous employ since then 
The opposite party by their letter dated 8th March, 1965 dismissed the petitioner 
from service alleging that he had committed certain misconduct- He has alleged 
that the management had not given him any opportunity to show cause against 
the charges. He was a workman concerned in industrial dispute Ref. No- CGIT 32 
of 1963. The employers also did not pay him one month’s wages npr did they 
make any application for approval. The dismissal was Illegal as the employers 
had contravened the provisions of section 33 Of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 
and he should be reinstated- 

3. After this complaint dated 27th June, 1965 notices were Issued to the parties 
but the companv did not file any written statement and the matter was adjourned 
from time tc time- Even at the hearing fixed in Ernakulam on 4th March, 1968 
both parties were absent but Shri T. C- N- Menon, Advocate, who had filed the 
original complalnl on behalf of the workmen informed the Tribunal that he had 



Sec. S(ii)j THE GAZETTE OF INDIA: JUNE 8. 19U8/.]iYAISTHA 18. 189(1 2 fy)l 


heard that the workmen had compromised the matter. However, again notices 
were sent and the complaint was fixed for hearing at Ernakulam on 22nd April, 
1908. Both parties were served with the notices of the hearing but they have 
remained absent and the matter is proceeded e.v parte . The complaint filed by 
Sbri Velu was In respect of his alleged dismissal from service. In spite of several 
notices he has remained absent which shows that he is not interested In ihe 
proceeding and it shall have to be held that he Is not entitled to any relief and 
hence my award accordingly. 

Ho order as to costs. 


Sd/- A- T. Zambue, 

Presiding Officer,

by the Central Government on 24th May, 1968. 

BEFORE THE CENTRAL GOVERNMENT INDUSTRIAL TRIBUNAL, BOMBAY

AND

STATE: Maharashtra- INDUSTRY: Major Ports and Docks. 

Dated 26th April 1968 
AWARD 

The Government of India in the Ministry of Labour and Employment by their 
Order No. 28|139|64!LRIV dated 21st January 1966 have referred to this Tribunal 
an industrial dispute existing between ihe employers in relation to the Trustees 
of the Port of Bombay and their workmen in respect of the matters specified 
in the following Schedule:— 

Schedule 

“Whether the promotion of Shri D. D. Bendkbale to the post of electrician 
grade 1 on the MPV Venui in the place of Shri N. G. Patil is justified? 
If not to what relief, if any, is Shri N. G. Patil entitled sr.d from 
what dote?" 

2. The circumstances giving rise to [he dispute may be stated in 'brief as 
follows : — 

The employers, the Trustees of the Port of Bombay had on or about the 7th 
of June 1962 commissioned the Motor Pilot Vessel ‘Venu’ and put it into service. 
By their Resolution No, T.R, 569, dated 19th June, 1962 they had for this vessel 
sanctioned some staff including three electricians and three wiremen and hence 
the electric foreman of the Southern Division of the employers had .ssued a 



2698 


THE GAZETTE OF IXDIA : JUNE 8, 1968/J1YAISTHA 18. 1890 [Part IX- 


notice inviting applications from the electricians and wiremen of both the electri- 
cal sections for the post of electricians and wiremen In this vessel. At about 
that time the workman Shrl D. D. Bendkhale who was holding the necessary 
qualification of first-class P.W.D. supervisor’s certificate was working as electri- 
cian grade II in the electrical establishment of the Bombay Port Trust and was 
posted at Wadala, while Shrl N. G. Patil the other workman had only appeared 
for the examination for the necessary qualification and was working as a wire- 
man in the electrical establishment in Southern Division. As per the notice Shri 
D. D. Bendkhale had submitted his application for the post on the pilot vessel 
through the head time-keeper. Shri N. G. Patil had made two applications. 
There were other applications also and ultimately Shri N. G, Patil who passed 
the examination for the necessary qualification came to be appointed and was 
directed to report himself from 13th August, 1962 to the post of electrician on the 
MPV Venu. 


3. It appears that in spite of applications of the candidates for the post of 
electrician grade l on the MPV Venu and the appointment of Shri N, G. Patil 
the result of the selection was not declared and Shri Bendkhale did not come to 
know that Shri Patil was selected for the post and that he had joined it, On 
oral enquiries Shri Bendkhale was told that the selection would be according 
to merits and seniority. He was working in the Northern Division of the Depart- 
ment at Wadala and was completely in dark about the appointment. On the 27th 
September, 11X13 when he, was sent to the M.P.V. Venu in leave vacancy to work 
there as an electrician 1st grade he came to know about the selection and appoint- 
ment of the junior person Shri N. G. Patil. In fact Shri Patil had passed the* 
second class certificate examination for being appointed us electrician grade II 
onlv in the month of July, 1962 that is after the last date for receiving the appli- 
cations for the post of the electrician on the vessel VENU. On the date of appli- 
cations he did not possess the requisite qualification for the post notified and 
hence Shri Bendkhale raised a dispute through the, Employees’ Union and made- 
representations to the authorities about the injustice done to him. 

4 The Chairman of the Bombay Port Trust, thereafter made enquiries and came 
to a finding that Shrl D. D. Bendkhale who was originally appointed as electrician 
grade II had .for the post of electrician grade I on the MPV Venu a claim superior 
to Shri N G. Patil who was onlv a wireman on the. material date. The applica- 
tion ol Shri Bendkhale had mysteriously disappeared. The Chairman therefore 
reverted Shri Patil and transferred him to the electrical, establishment and 
appointed Shri D. D. Bendkhale as an electrician grade I on the MPV Venu. 
Alter this appointment Shri Patil made representations through the B.P.T, General 
Workers’ Union who raised this dispute. They had also approached the Concilia- 
tion Officer and after the failure report it was referred to this Tribunal. 


5 The B.P.T. General Workers’ Union representing the case of Shrl N. G. 
Patil in whose place Shri D. D. Bendkhale was promoted have by their statement 
of claim contended that as per the notice inviting applications the right to apply 
for the post was not restricted only to electricians grade II but the appointments 
were thrown open to all employees of the Port Trust working, as wireman as well 
as assistant wireman and the only requirement that the applicant should possess 
was the necessary qualification needed to work as electrician grade I. It is alleged 
that tome four nersons who had applied for the post and were selected refused 
to accept the transfer on the vessel VENU because of the service conditions and 
thereafter Shri Patil who had passed the examination held by the Maharashtra 
Government in July, 1962 was selected by the Deputy Conservator tc .work as 
electrician grude I and was appointed to do that work on the MPV VENU with 
effect from 14th August 1962. Thus Patil was appointed by the authorities through 
the process of selection and as he had been working on the MPV Venn with 
effect from 14th August, 1962 upto 29th June, 1964 he should have been considered 
permanent and confirmed against that post and his demotion was riot justified. 

fl. As regards the claim made by Shrl D. D. Bendkhale the B.P-T- General 
Workers’ Union has alleged that there was no record available to show that Shri 
Bendkhale had made an application for the post- He had also not raised any 
objection or made any protest against the appointment of Shri Patil and the, sug- 
gestion that his application was suppressed by somebody was far fetched. They 
have contended that It, was only in the early part of 1964 that the B.P.T. 
Employees’ Union had demanded the promotion and transfer of Shri Bendkhale 
on MPV VENU and approached the then General Manager of the Bombay Port 
Trust Shrl Simoes on the subject. It Is alleged that as Shri SImoes was favour- 
ably inclined towards the B.P.T. Employees’ Union he had ignored the require- 
ments of law and had Issued orders immediately for the reversion of Shri N. G. 
Patil and the promotion of Shrl Bendkhale on the M-P.V- VENU. No employee

working under the Chief Engineer, B.P.T., in the electrical establishment had any 
claim whatsoever on the post of electrician Grade I on the MJP.V- VENU on the 
basis of seniority either on the length of service or on any other standard. Shrl 
Bendkhale was not entitled to be promoted in place of Shri Patil and by promoting 
Him the Bombay Port Trust has shown favouritism and unfair labour practice- 
There was absolutely no Justification for the promotion of Shrl Bendkhale and 
this Tribunal should direct the authorities to restore Shrl Patil to his former 
position 


7. Slui D. D. Bendkhale is represented by the B.P.T- Employees’ Union who

had sent his application within time on the 11th June 1982 through proper channel. 
The other candidate Shri N. G. Patil had not also the necessary qualification at

him to the post of electrician Grade I was unjust. They have further contended

khale was completely in the dark about the appointment of Shrl N- G. Patil; till

and explained to him about the wrongful promotion of Shri N. G. Patil ignoring

was .-of traceable. Thereafter his case was taken up by the union and the union 

made representations to the employers on his behalf and the Chairman recognised 
his superior claims and his promotion was justified. 


fi. Tha employers have by their statement supported the claim of Shri D. D.

employee can have no claim to the post.. ^

Shri Bendkhale had a superior claim over that of Shri N. G- Patii and the General 
Manager 1 had taken a just and proper decision in appointing Shri D- D, 'Bendkhale 
as electrician and In reverting Shri N. G. Patii; that it was neither any unfair 
labour practice nor nepotism or favouritism. On the contrary the injustice caused 
was undone and the promotion of Shri D. D. Bendkhale was justified. 

11. The reference perlains to a dispute regarding the promotion of an employee 
.and so the Legal Adviser on behalf of the management has contended that as 
promotion is only a managerial function this Tribunal has no jurisdiction to deal 
tvith the same- He has relied upon the ruling reported in Brooke Bond case 1963 
JLLJ p.256 and has argued that the dispute involves a pure question of promotion 
and it will not be open to this Tribunal to adjudicate upon it- I have already 
quoted the terms of the reference in the schedule by which this Tribunal has been 
asked to decide the question whether the promotion of Shri D- D. Bendkhule to the 
-post of electrician grade I on M-P.V- VENU in place of Shri N- G. Patii is justified 
and for deciding the same the Tribunal shall have t° consider the comparative 
merits and other factors of the two candidates. However, I do not think that on 
■that account only the matter will be taken out of the jurisdiction of the Tribunal. 
In the very ruling Brooke Bond case relied upon by the management the question 
involved was one of promotion and their Lordships have given the general formula 
jin respect of promotions in the following terms: — 

“All things being equal seniority shall count for promotion. If the senior 
person has been overlooked in the question of promotion he is at 
liberty to ask the concern for reason why he has been overlooked In 
which case the concern shall give him the reasons.” 

jin this ruling it has been further observed: — 

“It is true that though promotions would normally be a part of the manage- 
ment's function if it appears that in promoting one employee in pre- 
ference to another the management has been actuated by malicious 
considerations or that the failure to promote one eligible person 
amounts to an unfair labour practice, that would be a different matter- 
But in the absence of mala fldes normally it must be left to the dis- 
cretion of the management to select which of the employees should 
be promoted at a given time subject of course to the formula to which 
we have just referred." 

From these observations it is clear that though promotiofi is the function of the 
management the Tribunal can in certain cases transfer in the action taken by 
the management and I shall now discuss the evidence about the relative merits 
of the candidates and whether there was any unfair labour practice or mala fides 
in the promotion of Shri Bendkhale 

12. The employers had by their order transferred Shri N. G. Patii to the 
electrical department and had promoted Shri D. D. Bendkhale to the post of 
electrician grade I on the M P-V. VENU after which the BP.T. General Workers’ 
Union had made representations to the management on behalf of Shri N. G. Patii 
contending that Shri N- G. Patii was appointed to the post of electrician grade I 
■on the VENU with effect from 14th August. 1962. He was working against the 
permanent post on the vessel continuously and as the General Manager Shri 
Simoes was favourably inclined towards the B.P.T. Employees’ Union he ignored 
the requirements of the law and immediately issued orders for the reversion of 
Shri N. G. Patii and the promotion of Shri D. D. Bendkhale on the pilot vessel; 
and the Bombay Port Trust was guilty of practising discrimination and encourag- 
ing inter-union rivalries, and thereby provocating trade disputes. I have already' 
observed that both Shri D. D- Bendkhale and Shri N. G. Patii were in the employ 
of the Port Trust and were working in the electric department- It is not in dis- 
pute that the electric foreman had issued a notice inviting applications for the 
post and the last date for the receipt of applications was 18th June, 1962- It is 
also not in dispute that long before this notice Shri D. D. Bendkhale was working 
as electriciun grade II and was holding the requisite qualification for the appoint- 
ment to the post of electrician grade I, It is clear from the record that he had 
passed the examination for the first class certificate for P-W.D. in the year 1960 
and was qualified for the appointment while Shri N. G. Patii was working merely 
as a wireman on the day when the notice Inviting the applications for the post 
was issued. At that time he had only appeared for the examination of the first- 
class wireman’s certificate. Exhibit 5 which is the certificate produced by him is 
dated 17th July, 1962 and it is clear that till the last date for submission of 
applications for the post Shri Patii did not have even the necessary qualification 
for making an application and being appointed to the post.

n It is not in dispute that the channel of promotion in the engineering and 
port department is that an assistant wireman is to he promoted as wireman and 
the wireman is thereafter promoted as electrician grade II who will be further 
eligible to bo promoted as electrician grade I on the Pilot Vessel- It is clear from 
the ext -act exhibit E .3 that the method of recruitment of electrician on the M.P .V 
VENU is by promotion of an electrician grade II on the basis of senionty-cwm- 
buitabilitv It is further clear that only a person who holds a first class certificate 
P W D and is working as an electrician grade II is eligible for promotion to the 
post of electrician on the pilot vessel. Considering these requirements it is clear 
that Slin D D. Bendkhale was eligible for promotion subject to other conditions 
regarding seniority and suitability. 


14. However, the case of Shri N. G- Patil is quite different. I have already 
observed that he had not the necessary qualification for appointment to the post 
of electrician grade T- Not only he was not qualified but as he was not working 
as electrician "grade II he was also not eligible for that promotion. Shii D. D 
Bcndkhal' was working as electrician grade I. He was senior, qualified and 
eligible „nd il shall have to be held that Shri Bendkhale had far superior claims 
to the post in comparison To the claim of Shri N. G Patil- In fact considering 
the requirement it shall have to be held that Shri Patil had absolutely no claim 
to the post. He was neither qualified for making the application nor wus he 
eligible. He was not also senior and it is very surprising that the officer concerned 
should forward his name overriding the claims of others- 


15 Shri S. Maitra the General Secretary of the Bombay Port Trust General 
Workers Union has contended that according to the notification the management 
had thrown upon the post to wiremen as well as electricians grade II. Shri Patil 
was a wireman and had made the application. He was selected and was also 
appointed to the post and had worked there about a year and a half and the sub- 
sequent action of the management in reverting him and promoting Shri Bendkhale 
was nol lustified. I do not find any substance in the contention Considering 
the notice issued by the management it cannot be said that the post of the electri 
( ian grade 1 on the M P.V VENU was thrown open even to wlrcmen. I have 
already mentioned from the extracts exhibit E-3 the method of recruitment to the 
post of electrician grade I on the Pilot Vessel which shows Lhat it is by promotion 
of the electrician grade II on the basis of seniorlly-cum-suitability. It cannot be 
read in the notice exhibit £-1 that the post of electrician grade I on the vessel 
was thrown open to wiremen. It is significant to remember that by the notice 
applications were invited for two categories of appointments — one for the post of 
electrician grade 1 and one for tile post ol wireman The notice reads: - 

‘Applications are invited for two posts of electricians grade I (Rs. 125— 
(5 — 155 — EB — H — 185 (C.C- scale) equivalent Second Pay Commission 
scale Rs. 175 — 6 — 205—7—240 and one post of wireman grade (1) Rs. 
76 — 5 — 105, (2) 100 — 5 — 150 (C & C scale) equivalent Second Pay 
Commission Grade (I) Rs. 125 — 3—131 — 4—155, (2) Rs 150 — 5 — 180 
for Pilot Vessel D.P.V, VENU belonging to the Deputy Conservator 
from elect iclans and wireman or assistant wiremen of both the 
electrical sections possessing requisite qualifications.” 


This notice shows that the assistant wireman was to apply for the post of wireman 
and the c-.itention that even assistant wiremen could have applied for the post 
of electrician grade I does net stand to reason. 


16 The contention that Shri Patil was appointed on regular selection after the 
applications is not also supported by any record- The management has contended 
that it was an ad hnc appointment- It appears that there was neither any test 
nor a>\y interview and Shri Patil was transferred from the engineering department 
to the Port Department, i have already observed that the last date for submitting 
applications as per the notice was 18th June, 1962. It is not in dispute that Shri 
Patil had nol male any application for the post before this date- It is clear from 
exhibit E-H that he first made an application on 19th July, 1962 and the second 
application on 26th July, 1962. His first application is for the post of electrician 
2 nd grade and by file second application he had requested that he should be 
appointed as electrician on the M.P V. VENU- These applications cannot be said 
to have been made in the regular course as per the notification and it appears that 
alter file expiry of the date the officer concerned took these applications from Shri 
Patil and considered his case with special favour. Neither the B.P T. General 
Workers’ Union nor Shri Patil has produced the order of his appointment to the 
post of electrician grade I on M.P V- VENU end it has been argued that no order 
was issued to him but onlv a memo was sent. The employers have produced the 



2702 


THE GAZETTE OF INDIA: JUNE 8, J 968/JIYAISTHA 18. 1890 [Part II 


letter of the Mechanical Superintendent to the Deputy Conservator dated 11th 
August, 1962 exhibit E-2 in which he has stated:— 

“In continuation of my above I have to Inform you that Shri Narayan 
Ganpat Patil wireman electrical establishment S.D. who holds the 
necessary qualification ot first-class P.W.D. supervisor’s certificate pre- 
scribed for the post of electrician is being directed to report to you 
from 13th August, 1962 for appointment in the vacant post of electri- 
cian on the above vessel for which he has put in an application to 
the undersigned,” 

No appointment order had been issued to Shri Patil and there is no reason to dis- 
believe the management’s contention that the appointment of Shri N. G. Patil 
waa on an ad hoc basis by mere transfer and the contention of the Bombay Port 
Trust General Workers’ Union that he was regularly selected and was appointed 
to the permanent post cannot be accepted. 

17. The B.P.T- Employees’ Union has contended that not only Shri D. D, 
Bendkhale was qualified for appointment to the post of electrician grade I on the 
M.P-V. VENU but he had also made an application in time- According to them 
his application was suppressed and the officer favouring Shri Patil appointed him. 
It Is clear that Shri Bendkhale made a representation and a complaint to the 
management in October 1963 when he was posted at the M.P.V. VENU in the 
leave vacancy. After this complaint the management had made enquiries about 
hie original application and it is clear from the statement of Shri Rabadi dated 
13th December, 1903 that Shri Bendkhale had made an application. It is also 
clear that he had made a note about that application in the muster book. Shri 
Rabadi is a chargeman at Wadala, Shri Shetve for the B.P.T. Employees’ Union 
has contended that Shri Rabadi is a member of the B.P.T. General Workers’ Union 
and he would not tell a lie in favour of Shri Bendkhale who was a member of the 
rival employees’ union. The statement of Shri Rabadi given in 1963 proves that 
Shri Bendkhale had made the application within time. Shri Bendkhale had the 
necessary qualification. He had made the application and it is clear that he was 
also entitled to get the post in preference to Shri Patil. 

18. It is true that Shri N- G, Pafil worked at the post to which he was trans- 
ferred from 14th August, 1962 to June 1963. However, this circumstance in itself 
will not entitle him to any claim to that post. I have already observed that no 
order of appointment has been issued in favour to Shri Patil- If we consider the 
terms and conditions of service stated in the notice it is clear that the appoint- 
ment was to be on a temporary basis. In the notice itself it has been stated — 

“The selection will be made from candidates possessing requisite qualifica- 
tions according to the seniority- 

The selected employees will be required to carry out their duties as per 
service condition shown below and as may be changed or required by 
the Dy. Conservator from time to time. 

The selected candidates will be appointed on a temporary basis in the first 
instance. They may however be posted permanently if found suitable 
and will be transferred to the Dy. Conservator Department. On such 
transfer being effected such employees will have no lien or claim for 
promotion to higher posts in the electrical section ”. 

Thus it is clear that the appointment of the selected candidate also would be on a 
temporary basis. Secondly- the nost itself was of a temporary nature. I hnve 
already observed that the trustees of the Port of Bombay had commissioned the 
vessel M- P. V. Venu and had sanctioned some staff on a provisional basis. The 
excerpts from the proceedings of the meeting of the trustees held on 19tb June 
1962, exhibit E-7 para 25 states:— 

“It is expected that there will consequently be scope for an appreciable 
reduction in the crew of the M. P. V. Venu after sufficient experience 
has been gained on working it. Accordingly all staff proposals are 
provisional and are subject to review " 

Thus it is clear that the post itself was temporary. .The appointment was also 
to be made on a temporary basis and the contention of the B.P.T. General Workers’ 
Union that Shri Patil was working, against a permanent post does not stand to 
reason and in view of the temporary nature of the post Shri Patil cannot be 
said to have any claim over It- The learned Legal Adviser has invited my attention 
to the Supreme Court ruling reported in 1867 1 LLJ page 708(7121, It has been 
held by this Court that when a first appointment or promotion is made on 



Sec. S(U)] THE GAZETTE OF INDIA ; JUNE 8, 1968/JYAI3THA 18. 1890 2703 


probation for a specific period and the employee is allowed to continue in the post 
after the exfilry of the said period without any specific order of confirmation be 
continues as a probationer only and acquires no substantive right to hold the post. 
In view of this ruling also it shall have to be held that Shri Patil had no claim 
of permanency over the post and the management had every right to transfer him 
to the original department. 

19. The General Secretary of the Bombay Port Trust General Workers’ Union 
has very seriously criticised the conduct of the management in promoting Shri 
Bendkhale and has alleged that the General Manager had practised discrimination 
and encouraged Inter-union rlvarly. It has been argued that Shri Patil belonged 
to the B-P.T. General Workers' Union while Shri Bendkhale was a member of the 
B.P.T. Employees Union and as the General Manager Shri Simoes was favourably 
inclined to the B-P.T. Employees’ Union he favoured Shri Bendkhale and promoted 
him to the post in place of Shri Patil. Shri Shetye the General Secretary of the 
B P-T. Employees’ Union has contended that both Shri Patil and Shri Bendkhale 
were members of his union but after the representation of Shri Bendkhale against 
Shri Patil Shri Patil left the B.P.T. Employees’ Union and joined the B.P.T. General 
Workers’ Union and there was no question of encouragement of Interunion rivalry 
by the management. He has further argued that Shri Bendkhale had a superior 
claim to the post. Shri Patil was ineligible and unqualified and was appointed 
to the post even though he had not made any application in time. The management 
had merely undone the injustice that was caused to Shri Bendkhale because of the 
loss of his application and there was no question of any favouritism- In view of 
Jhe circumstance that both Shri Bendkhale and Shri Patil formerly belonged to 
the B.P.T- Employees’ Union and that Shri Patil Joined the B.P.T- General 
Workers’ Union subsequently after the complaint by the Employees' Union on 
behalf of Shri Bendkhale I do not find any substance In the grievance that the 
management was indulging in inter-union rivalry or was showing favouritism. 
Had the management been favouring the B P.T. Employees’ Union Shri Bendkhale 
would not have been required to suffer for such a long time in the whole affair. 

20, I have already discussed the merits of the two candidates whose promotion 
is the subject matter of this reference. There is overwhelming evidence to show 
that Shri Bendkhale had superior claims to Shri Patil for the post of electrician 
grade 1, on the M.F.V. Venu. The circumstances also clearly show in at though 
Shri Patil did not even make an application some officer who was interested in 
him had favoured him by taking the application and transferring him to the 
Deputy Conservator’s Department The claims of Shri D. D. Bendkhale were 
Ignored and by the subsequent order the management had undone the injustice 
caused to him. There is no question of any unfair labour practice or main fides 
in the promotion of Shri Bendkhale and the action of the management in promoting 
Shri Bendkhale is justified. In view of this finding the question of any relief to 
Shri N, G. Pati does not arise. Hence my award accordingly. 


No order as to costs 


Sd/- A. T. Zamerf., 


Central Government Industrial 


Presiding Officer 
Tribunal, Bombay,

S.O. 2001. — In pursuance of the provisions of bye-law XI (4) of the Bye-laws: 
of the National Council for Safety in Mines, the Central Government hereby 
publishes the audited accounts of receipts and expenditure of the National 
Council for Safety in Mines for the period from the 1st April, 1966 to the 31st 
March, 1967 alongwith the Auditor’s Report. 

Audit Report on the accounts of the National Council for Safety in Mines, Dhanbad , 
for the year ending 31st March, 1967. 

1 . The audit of the accounts of the National Council for Safety in Mines, Dhan- 
had for the period from 1st April 1966 to 31st March 1987, was conducted by the 
Accountant General, Bihar in accordance with clause XI (2) of the Bye-laws of the 
Council. 

2. A statement of Income and Expenditure for the year 1966-67 and Balance

on the ground that the Central Government was satisfied, that the licence would 
not serve the purpose for which it had been granted, in terms of Clause 9, sub- 
clause (cc).

with the remarks of the postal authorities “Intentionally refused”.

S.O. 2011, — M/s. K. B. Thaker & Co., 110, Commerce House. 140. Medows 
Street, Bombay-1 were granted an import licence No. P/NA/AD/2016837/C/XX/24/ 
C/H/21-22/NQQ dated 21st March, 1967 for Rs. 15,866/- (Rupees Fifteen Thousand 
Eight Hundred and Sixty Six only.) for the Import of motor vehicle parts etc. bs 
per list attached thereto. The licensee has now applied to this office for the issue 
of a duplicate Customs Purposes copy of the said licence on the ground that the 
original Customs Purposes copy of the licence in question, has been misplaced/ lost- 
It is further stated that the original Customs Purposes copy of the licence has been 
registered with Bombay Port Customs House and has been utilised to the extent 
of Rs- 8,966/— The duplicate Customs Purposes copy of the licence now required 
by the licencee is for the value of Rs, 11,900/ -. 

In support of their contention the licensee have filed an affidavit- I am accord- 
ingly satisfied that the original Customs Purposes copy of the said licence has been 
lost. Thersfore, in exercise of the powers conferred under sub-clause 9(CC) of 
the Imports (Control) Order, 1955 dated 2nd December, 1955 (as amended), the 1 
original Customs Purposes copy of the licence No. P/NA/ AD/201 6837/C/XX/24/ 
C/H/21-22/NQQ dated 21st March, 1967 Issued to M/s. K. B. Thaker & Co., 
HO, Commerce House, 140, Medows Street, Bombay-1- Is hereby cancelled. 

-A duplicate Customs Purposes copy of the said licence is being issued.

MINISTRY OF PETROLEUM & CHEMICALS 
(Department of Petroleum) 

New Delhi, the 28th May 1968 


8.0- 2016 Whereas by a notification of the Government of India in the Min- 

istry of Petroleum and Chemicals S.O, No. 3323, dated 2nd September, 1967, under 
sub-section (1) of Section 3 of the Petroleum Pipelines (Acquisition of Right of 
User in Land) Act, 1962 (50 of 1962), the Central Government declared its inten- 
tion to acquire the right of user in the lands specified in the schedule appended 
to that notification for the purpose of laying pipelines. 

And whereas the competent authority has, under sub-section (1) of section 6 
of the said Act, submitted report to the Government; 

And whei'eas, the Central Government has, after considering the said report, 
decided to acquire the right of user in the lands specified in the schedule 
appended to this notification. 


Now, whereas, in exercise of the power conferred by sub-section (1) of the 
Section 6 of the the said Act, the Central Government hereby declares that the 
right of user in the said lands specified in the schedule appended to this notifica- 
tion is hereby acquired for laying the pipelines and in exercise of the powers 
conferred by sub-scction (1) of that section, the Central Government directs that 
the right of user in the said lands shall instead of vesting in the Central Govern- 
ment, vest on this date of. the publication of this declaration in the Oil and Natu- 
ral Gas Commission free from all encumbrances.

S.O. 2017 Whereas it appears to the Central Government that it ia necessary

S.O. 2018. — Whereas by a notification of the Government of India In the Ministry 
of Petroleum and Chemicals S-O. No. 1264 dated 25th March 1968 under sub-section 
[Cl) of Section 3 of the Petroleum Pipelines (Acquisition of Right of User in land)’ 
Act, 1962 (50 of 1902), the Central Government declared its intention to acquire the 
Right of User in the lands specified in the schedule appended to that notification 
for the purpose of laying pipelines. 

And whereas the Competent Authority has, under sub-section (1) of section 6 of 
the said Act, submitted report to the Government; 

And whereas, the Central Government has, after considering the said report, 
decided to acquire the right of user in the lands specified in the schedule appended 
to this notification- 

Now, whereas, in exercise of the powers conferred by sub-section (1) of the 
Section 6 of the said Act, the Central Government hereby declares that the right of 
user in the said lands specified in the schedule appended to this notification Is 
hereby acquired for laying the pipelines and in exercise of the powers conferred by 
sub-section (4) of that Section, the Central Government directs that the right of 
user In the said lands shall instead of vesting in the Central Goverment, vest on 
this date of the publication of this declaration in the Oil and Natural Gas Commla- 
slon free from all encumbrances.

ORDER 

New Delhi, the 27th May 1968 

S.O. 2020 — In exercise of the powers conferred by section 18-G of the Industriea 
(Development and Regulation) Act, 1951 (65 of 1951), the Central Government 
hereby makes the following order to amend the Ethyl Alcohol (Price Control) Order, 
1966 namely: — 

1. This Order may be called the Ethyl Alcohol (Price Control) Amendment 
Order, 1968. 

2. In the Ethyl Alcohol (Price Control) Order, 1966, after clause 3, the following 
clause shall be inserted, namely: — 

"3. A- Authority to determine the additional charges. — (1) In case any doubt or 
dispute arises as regards the additional charges leviable under clause 
3, it shall be decided by the Excise Commissioner of the State where 
the particular distillery ig located- 

(2) Before taking the decision referred to in sub-clause (1), the Excise Com- 

missioner shall given adequate opportunity to the parties concerned to 
present their case. 

(3) The Excise Commissioner shall, for the purpose of determining the addi- 

tional charges, have power to call upon the seller and the buyer to 
furnish such documentary evidence as may be considered necessary by 
him for the purpose ”

MINISTRY OF FOOD, AGRICULTURE, COMMUNITY DEVELOPMENT AND 

COOPERATION 

(Department of Agriculture) 

New Delhi , the 27 th May 1968 

S-O. 2028— The following draft rules further to amend the Curry Powder 
Grading and Marking Rules, 1956, which the Central Government proposes to make 
in exercise of the powers conferred by section 3 of the Agricultural Produce (Grad- 
ing and Marking) Act, 1937 are published as required by the said section for the 
Information of all persons likely to be affected thereby and notice is hereby given 
that the said draft will be taken into consideration on or after 1st July 1968. 

Any objections and suggestions which may be received from any person with 
respect to the said draft before the date so specified will be considered by the 
Central Government. 


Draft Rules 

1. These rules may be called the Curry Powder Grading and Marking (Amend- 
ment) Rules, 1968. 

2- In the Curry Powder Grading and Marking Rules, 1956, 

(a) for rules 5 and 6, the following rules shall be substituted, namely: — 

“5. Grade Designation Marks.-— (1) The grade designation marks in the case 
of curry powder packed in ploythene or paper bags shall consist of 
a design incorporating the number of Certificate of Authorisation, 
the word ‘Agmark’ and the grade approved by the Agricultural Mar- 
keting Adviser. 

(2) The grade designation mark in the case of curry powder packed in tins 
or glass containers shall consist of a paste-on label specifying the 
grade designation and bearing the design of a map of India with the 
word 'Agmark’- 

.(3) The grade designation mark in the case of curry powder packed in con- 
tainers of Jute or cloth as also in containers in which sealed ploythene 
bags of graded curry powder are packed shall consist of a label, speci- 
fying the grade designation and bearing the design (consisting of an 
outline map of India with the word ‘Agmark’ and the figure of the 
rising sun, with the words ‘Produce of India’ and TfUT” 

resembling the one as set out in Schedule I. 

8. Method of Marking. — (1) The grade designation mark shall be securely 
affixed to or printed on each container in a manner approved by the 
Agricultural Marketing Adviser. 


*0.8(11)] the GAZETTE OF INDIA: JUNE 8, I968/JIYAISTHA 18. 1890 


2729 


(2) In addition to the above, the following particulars shall also be clearly 

and indelibly marked on each container namely: 

(a) Date of packing in code or plain letters. 

(b) Lot number, and 

(c) Net weight. 

(3) An authorised packer may, after obtaining the prior approval of the 

Agricultural Marketing Adviser, mark his private trade mark on a 
container, in a manner approved by the said officer, provided that the 
private trade mark does not represent a quality or grade different 
from that indicated by the grade designation mark, affixed to or printed 
on the container in accordance with the rules.” 

(b) In rule 7, for sub-rule (1) the following sub-rule shall be substituted, 
namely: — 

"(1) Only new and clean tins, bottles and polythene or Jute or cloth con- 
tainers or paper bags properly lined with suitable material shall bo 
used for packing curry powder and these shall be closed and sealed 
in a manner approved by the Agricultural Marketing Adviser to the 
Government of India.” 

[No. F. 13-20/67-AM-J

(Department of Agriculture) 

New Delhi, the 28 th May 1968 

S-O. 2030 — In exercise of the powers conferred by clause (b) of sub-section (2)' 
of section 3 of the Essential Commodities Act, 1955 (10 of 1955), the Central Gov- 
ernment hereby makes the following Order to amend the Arable Land (Utilisation 
for Food Crops) Order, 1963, namely: — 

1. This Order may be called the Arable Land (Utilisation for Food Corps)' 
Amendment Order, 1968. 

2. In the Arable Land (Utilisation for Food Corps) Orders, 1963, for the proviso 
to sub-clause (1) of clause 3, the following proviso shall be substituted, namely:— 

"Provided that the Collector shall not Issue such notice in respect of any land; 

(a) which is used and maintained as grass land and is classified as such 

in the revenue records; and 

(b) which has been continuously used as grass land for a period of not less 

than four years Immediately preceding the date of notice, whether 
or not it was recorded as such in the revenue records; 

Provided further that the Collector shall not issue such notice in respect ol 
any land which, under any law for the time being in force, is reserved in the year 
Immediately proceeding the date of the notice for any of the folowing purposes 
namely: — 

(a) pasture land; 

(b) threashing floor; 

(c) irrigation tank; or 

(d) any other public purpose. 

Explanation : — For the purposes of this clause “Grass land” means any land in 
the private ownership of an Individual in which grass or fodder trees or both 
are grown.

his report to the Central Government ; 

And Whereas the Central Government after considering the report and after consulting 
the Government of Bihar is satisfied that — 5

described in Schedule ‘A’ appended hereto ; and 1 ' ' 

(b) the rights to mine, quarry, bore, dig and search for, win, work and carry away minerals

should be acquired.

MINISTRY OF INDUSTRIAL DEVELOPMENT AND COMPANY AFFAIRS 
(Department of Industrial Development) 

ORDER 

New Delhi, the 27 th May 1968 

S-O- 2034/IDRA/18G/68. — In exercise of the powers conferred by sections 
18G and 25 of the Industries (Development and Regulation) Act. 1961 (65 of 1951), 
and all other powers enabling it in that behalf, the Central Government hereby 
makes the following Order further to amend the Cement Control Order, 1967:— 

1- This Order may be called the Cement Control (Fourth Amendment) 
Order, 1988. 



Sec. 8(H)] THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JUNE 8, 1968/JlYAISTHA 18. 1890 


2735 

2. In the Cement Control Order, 1967, lor clause 9, the following clause shall 
be substituted, namely: — 

"9- Payments to Cevient Requlation Account .- — Every producer shall, in respect 
of each transaction by way of sale of cement eflected by him, pay within one 
month of the close of the month in which sales take place, to the Controller, an 
amount equivalent to the amount, if any, by which free on rail destination price 
of such cement released by him exceeds the aggregate of the following amounts, 
namely: — 

(i) the ex-factory price of such cement calculated in accordance with the 

rates specified in the Schedule; 

(ii) a selling agency commission calculated at the rate of Rs- 1-25 per tonne; 

(iii) the excise duty paid thereon; and 

(iv) in the case of packed cement, the charges fixed by the Central Govern- 

ment in respect of the packing or the containers under the first 
proviso to clause 8: 

Provided that the expenditure incurred by the producer on freight by the 
cheapest mode of transport or where any other mode of transport has been 
specified by the Central Government under 4, by such mode of transport in 
respect of such transactions shall be reimbursed to the producer by the Controller 
from out of the Cement Regulation Account referred to in clause II. 

Provided further that nothing contained in this clause shall apply to the 
cement moved out of the factories prior to the 1st day of January 1068 but sold 
-after that date”. 

[No F. 1-32/67-Cem. Vol. II.] 
K. I VIDYASAGAR. Jt Secy-

amendment In the notification of the Government of India, in the Ministry of

1968, published In the Gazette of India dated the Gth April, 1963, at Part II-Sec-
